    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 31.57%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.7 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.09 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 1.40 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 31.57%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 5.3 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency on 
          a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited     
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 42.9% of the total average of 12.4 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 22.49%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 23.9 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.5 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.34%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 13.1 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 2.08 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.09 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 36.71%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 10.0 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 36.7% of the total average of 27.1 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.01128%                                                                                        
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          enable the rule to provide more guidance.                                                                     
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 26.36%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 22.8 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 20.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.56%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 13.4 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 1.90 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.08 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 47.29%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.1 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 47.3% of the total average of 25.5 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.01105%                                                                                        
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 27.54%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 22.5 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 19.6 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          The difference between calculated theoretical (100.0%) and measured achieved occupancy (41.1%) can be the     
          result of warp scheduling overheads or workload imbalances during the kernel execution. Load imbalances can   
          occur between warps within a block as well as across blocks of the same kernel. See the CUDA Best Practices

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.17%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 12.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 1.98 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.09 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 38.94%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.9 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency on 
          a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited     
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 38.9% of the total average of 25.3 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.01218%                                                                                        
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    WRN   The optional metric smsp__pcsamp_sample_count could not be found. Collecting it as an additional metric could 
          enable the rule to provide more guidance.                                                                     
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 26.57%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 23.4 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 20.9 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.08%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 12.6 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 2.13 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.09 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 36.23%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.8 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency on 
          a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited     
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 36.2% of the total average of 27.0 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.01184%                                                                                        
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    WRN   The optional metric smsp__pcsamp_sample_count could not be found. Collecting it as an additional metric could 
          enable the rule to provide more guidance.                                                                     
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 28.87%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 22.3 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 19.5 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.74%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 13.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 1.82 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.08 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 41.31%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 10.3 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 41.3% of the total average of 25.0 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.012%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (73.1%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (73.1%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

    WRN   The optional metric smsp__pcsamp_sample_count could not be found. Collecting it as an additional metric could 
          enable the rule to provide more guidance.                                                                     
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 25.14%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 23.5 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.0 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.05%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 12.6 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 2.01 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.09 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 38.82%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.8 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency on 
          a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited     
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 38.8% of the total average of 25.3 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.01135%                                                                                        
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    WRN   The optional metric smsp__pcsamp_sample_count could not be found. Collecting it as an additional metric could 
          enable the rule to provide more guidance.                                                                     
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 29.85%                                                                                          
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 22.2 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 19.3 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 92.07%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 12.6 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to    
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 2.24 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.09 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 43.12%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.2 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency   
          on a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited  
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 43.1% of the total average of 28.3 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.                                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.01207%                                                                                        
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 31.6 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 21.1 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          The difference between calculated theoretical (100.0%) and measured achieved occupancy (11.4%) can be the     
          result of warp scheduling overheads or workload imbalances during the kernel execution. Load imbalances can   
          occur between warps within a block as well as across blocks of the same kernel. See the CUDA Best Practices
